# Dubon 2026 — acceptance checklist

Original finite quotients and symmetric-power parameter maps: Four further production modules prove that the actual kernel of an original continuous matrix representation over a discrete coefficient ring is open. For an original compact group, its actual kernel quotient is finite; the faithful quotient representation composes with the original quotient map to recover the original representation exactly. The concrete coordinate algebra of a finite group over a Noetherian coefficient ring is Noetherian by the pinned polynomial and quotient results. Consequently, each original continuous discrete representation has its exact evaluation from the actual Noetherian coordinate algebra of its own kernel quotient. Separately, evaluation of the actual symmetric power of the original universal rank-two representation constructs the genuine contravariant map on coordinate algebras. Its universal and original-evaluation identities are proved from the existing coefficient-change theorem. The actual symmetric power of a genuine first-order lift reduces correctly, preserves original strict conjugacy and equality of the original adjoint H1 classes, and retains continuity in the original topology. Four exact consumers expand registered scope to 1413 production modules, 780 exact consumers and 6,175 explicit audit registrations. Focused production compilation passed; source-consumer compilation, expanded audit, all sixteen linters and sequential foundation/paper verification are pending. The finite kernel quotient may vary with the original representation; no single finite quotient for all deformations is claimed. These finite-group coordinate algebras and genuine symmetric-power maps do not prove complete local profinite deformation or pseudodeformation representability, local p-adic Hodge conditions, Selmer finiteness or vanishing, Galois association, patching, higher automorphic lifts or analytic continuation. Unconditional Deligne, non-CM Sato–Tate and the final modular application remain open. DUB-01–16 remain accepted; DUB-17–20 remain OPEN. Earlier milestone paragraphs retain their historical scope.


Original representation coordinate algebra and its actual universal property: Four further production modules construct the actual polynomial coordinate algebra of matrix representations of the original group: one variable for every original matrix entry, with the ideal generated by the literal identity and multiplication equations. These equations are proved in the quotient. Its actual coordinate matrices form a genuine group homomorphism and hence are invertible, giving the actual universal representation. Evaluation at an original representation kills the entire relation ideal and descends to an algebra map; base change of the universal representation recovers that entire original representation. Conversely, evaluating a representation obtained from an original algebra map recovers the whole original algebra map. These two proved inverse constructions give a genuine equivalence over every coefficient algebra, and the coordinate evaluation is natural under coefficient-algebra maps. ## DUB-05 — Actual vertical Jessen identity

- [x] **DONE** — Prove existence of the fixed-N symmetric vertical log mean, its Haar identity, finiteness and convexity; control the unbounded negative logarithm, not merely continuous equidistribution.

`JessenMean.tendsto_verticalLogMean_haar` proves the fixed-N symmetric logarithmic mean equals the normalized Haar log integral for every real σ, assuming N≥1 and a₁≠0. The proof derives nonzero finite jets for every genuine prime twist, obtains a finite compact cover, proves a uniform sublevel measure estimate, integrates its exponential logarithmic deficit, and controls truncation errors uniformly over all T≥1. `jessenFunction` is defined by the actual vertical limit; `jessenFunction_eq_haar` and `jessenFunction_bounds` identify it and prove 0≤J≤log(E)/2 when a₁=1. `JessenConvexity` also proves convexity and continuity: finite product-grid averages converge uniformly for continuous torus tests, analytic products of actual prime twists satisfy Hadamard three-lines, and their Haar logarithms equal the number of factors times the original potential. `SemanticRegression.bohr_jessen_proposition_source` assembles the full Proposition 3.3 conclusion. The general zero-frequency formula is implemented in `GeneralZeroDensity`; modular concentration remains open, and DUB-05 is accepted after full source review and sequential verification. The logarithmic singularity is controlled by a proved uniform estimate. Dependencies: DUB-04. Source anchors: `prop:bohr-jessen`.

## DUB-06 — Vertical zero frequency with multiplicity

- [x] **DONE** — Prove the Jessen–Tornehave count limit for every open strip with correct one-sided derivatives, analytic multiplicities and symmetric height normalization 1/(2T).

`DirichletDivisor` and `RectangleZeroCount` consume the existing foundation’s `GuthMaynardExternal.PNT.RectangleArgumentPrinciple`: the divisor weights are proved equal to actual analytic multiplicities, and the normalized contour integral equals the literal open-rectangle count when the boundary is nonvanishing. `ZeroFreeBoundaries` derives arbitrarily high admissible heights from the actual countable zero set. `ZeroFreeHalfPlanes` places all zeros in a bounded vertical strip. `JensenZeroBound`, `UniformTwistZeroBound`, `VerticalZeroTranslation` and `UniformLocalZeroCount` derive a multiplicity bound uniform over every translated unit-height window, using actual prime twists and preserved analytic orders. `ZeroCountHeight` proves monotonicity and bounded unit changes of the height cutoff. `HeightLimitTransfer` derives a linear count bound and proves that moving to selected heights in [T,T+1] preserves a density limit. It also derives a zero-free contour selection and transfers a limit of its normalized contour integral to all heights; the contour limit is an explicit narrower hypothesis in that transfer theorem, now discharged at the regular endpoints described below. `RealExponentialZeros` proves the nonoscillation theorem by Rolle induction on the actual finite real exponential sum; `RealExponentialZeroCount` obtains a finite zero set. `HorizontalRealZeros` identifies the real part of the actual horizontal Dirichlet polynomial and proves it has fewer than N zeros at every height. `LogDerivativeHalfPlane` and `ZeroFreeRealSign` bound argument change by π on each half-plane segment; `FiniteCrossingArgument` combines the pieces using a deliberately coarse finite-crossing bound. `HorizontalArgumentBound` proves |Im ∫_l^u P′/P(σ+it) dσ| ≤ π·2^N on every nonvanishing horizontal segment, uniformly in height and endpoints. The source consumer unfolds the actual polynomial. `RectangleRealParts` and `ContourSlopeControl` identify the real contour components and bound the normalized count error by 2^N/(2T); they also prove the approximate monotonicity of the actual vertical logarithmic-derivative mean. `LogNormDerivative` and `FiniteHeightDerivative` justify its identity with the derivative of the finite-height logarithmic mean on zero-free vertical segments. `LocalVerticalJetCover`, `LocalVerticalSublevel` and `LocalLogTail` reuse the actual derivative and truncation estimates to obtain uniform logarithmic control near each abscissa. `FiniteHeightContinuity` proves continuity even when the segment contains zeros. `FiniteExceptionalMeanValue` and `FiniteVerticalExceptions` carry derivative inequalities across the genuine finite exceptional set. `FiniteHeightSecants`, `SecantLimit` and `SelectedMeanMotion` prove the vertical mean-motion limit on selected zero-free horizontal contours at differentiable zero-free abscissae. `RegularZeroDensity` combines this with the actual contour error and nearby-height count transfer: for l≤u with zero-free vertical lines and J differentiable at both endpoints, count(l,u,T)/(2T) tends to (J′(u)−J′(l))/(2π). The three additional source consumers unfold the finite polynomial, the finite-height integral and the contour error. `RegularAbscissae` and `RegularApproximation` derive dense differentiable zero-free abscissae and approximate every differentiable endpoint. `ZeroCountInterval` and `ContinuousEndpointZeroDensity` remove the zero-free boundary restriction by literal count monotonicity. `JessenAtomRegularity` identifies differentiability with absence of a Stieltjes atom; `AtomFreeZeroDensity` proves the open-strip count limit for every pair of atom-free endpoints. `TotalVerticalZeros` defines the actual total and outside-band multiplicity counts, and `TotalZeroDensity` proves total density log M/(2π) from enclosing regular strips and the actual derivative asymptotes. `OutsideZeroDensity` proves the normalized outside-band limit at atom-free boundaries. `BoundaryAtoms` proves the exceptional radii countable across every truncation. `ZetaZeroCount` proves Corollary 6.1 with this explicit boundary convention and the order of limits fixed: each N≥2 has its actual height limit before N tends to infinity. `ZeroCountRadius` and `ZetaCountTails` additionally prove iterated eventual smallness for every positive radius, including exceptional radii; this tail bound does not assert existence of the fixed-N limit at atoms. Four further source consumers unfold the finite zero sets, multiplicities, count sums and normalization. `GeneralZeroDensity` now proves the formula at atom-carrying boundaries; DUB-06 is accepted after the complete source review and 372-module verification. Dependencies: DUB-05. Source anchors: `prop:JT-density`.

## DUB-07 — Total mass and probability normalization

- [x] **DONE** — Construct J″ as the positive Stieltjes/distributional measure, prove total mass log M_N and eventual M_N>1, and check the 1+exp(−λs) normalization example.

`JessenStieltjes` constructs the positive Stieltjes measure of the actual convex Jessen function. `ConvexDerivatives` proves right continuity of the right derivative and identifies its left limit with the left derivative, so open intervals and atoms have the exact one-sided formulas. `LeadingBohrTerm` proves terminal-term dominance uniformly on the torus; `JessenLeftLimit` and `JessenRightLimit` derive both endpoint asymptotes and derivative limits. `JessenMass` proves total mass log M, the 1/(2π) scaling, probability normalization for M>1 and the zero measure for M=1. `PeriodicMean`, `BinomialJessen`, `BinomialMeasure`, `BinomialZeros` and `BinomialZeroCount` prove the source’s general λ>0 normalization example: the actual logarithmic limit is max(0,−λσ), its Stieltjes second derivative is λδ₀, the actual zeros are (2m+1)πi/λ with multiplicity one, and their symmetric density is λ/(2π). The example also has the exact open-strip count/measure limit, including endpoints at its atom. `IsolatedPrimeSupport` now derives eventual coefficient nonvanishing, N/2<M_N≤N, M_N>1, M_N→∞ and log M_N/log N→1 from the literal compact-interval comparability in H2 and growing isolated prime blocks. It consumes the proved mass theorem to obtain actual probability measures for all sufficiently large N. `GeneralZeroDensity` now proves the general multiplicity-weighted zero-frequency identity. `GeneralZeroDensity` supplies the separate identification with vertical zero frequencies; DUB-07 is accepted after the complete source review and 372-module verification. ### Actual full-level Rankin–Selberg three-fifths remainder — 7 October 2026

`LevelOneRankinSelberg.level_one_rankin_selberg` proves the literal normalized coefficient-square asymptotic with the actual positive Petersson residue for every nonzero full-level cusp form of integral weight at least two. The uniform bound also holds for zero forms. Its proof consumes the actual two-residue contour identity, full-level functional equation, polynomial strip estimate, vanishing horizontal edges, finite-contour Dirichlet-series interchange, uniformly dominated Gamma cutoff limit, sharp dual difference estimates, both positive Riesz unsmoothing inequalities and actual Möbius coefficient inversion. Neither the Rankin error nor a conclusion-equivalent arithmetic certificate is an input.

The left critical line is treated as a symmetric improper limit. It is never replaced by an unjustified whole-line Bochner integral. The all-cutoff bound is uniform in both the coefficient index and the height, so the interchange with the infinite coefficient sum does not rely on an index-dependent height threshold. Both forward and backward differences use the same floor(x^(3/5)) frequency split; the explicit x>=32 range is extended to every x>=1 using the proved finite coefficient bound. The generic convolution transfer consumes a truly convergent inverse series at 3/5 and its value at one, retaining the exact Petersson constant.

### Actual rescaled cusp traces and common-period Mellin identity — 7 October 2026

The original product-level cusp form is translated by the actual inverse diagonal matrix. The mixed subgroup has exactly the upper-right divisibility by a and lower-left divisibility by b; its real image is proved equal to the diagonal conjugate of Gamma0(ab), and it contains Gamma(ab). Restricting each genuine integral coset translate to the normal principal subgroup therefore supplies one common period ab. The finite sum retains every actual coset once. Its Petersson density is full-level invariant, so its horizontal square energy is one-periodic. Exact averaging over ab periods and actual Parseval identify that unit-interval integral with the complete finite family of q-expansion squares.

Every actual normalized period coefficient family has a proved linear mean. Summing the genuine finite family and convolving with the full-level principal square sequence preserves nonnegativity and the linear mean, giving absolute convergence for Re(s)>1. The exact Mellin transform has factor (4 pi / ab)^(-(s+k-1)) Gamma(s+k-1). Absolute Bochner integrability is proved by summable integral norms; no formal exchange of a divergent integral and series is used. The integer and projective coset domains have equal multiplicities because the mixed group absorbs the center, and diagonal transport of the original Gamma0 domain gives a second genuine domain for the same real projective group.

This is the actual coefficient and domain construction. The reflected lattice-cusp integral has not yet been identified with this coefficient series. That identification still needs the invariant completed-Eisenstein trace unfolding and the exact determinant/conductor factors. The general-level contour estimates and x^(3/5) conclusion remain OPEN. The already verified full-level Rankin–Selberg theorem and all weighted energy regimes remain intact. Deligne and unconditional Sato–Tate remain OPEN. No gate changes: 15/20 accepted.

### Actual reflected general-level cusp series — 7 October 2026

The mixed-domain integral is absolutely integrable by actual cusp decay on every finite coset tile. Hyperbolic measure preservation, disjointness of the genuine tiling and diagonal slash transport give the exact determinant factor a^(k-2). The finite Petersson trace is invariant under the whole modular group. Primitive full-level Eisenstein unfolding and the proved common-period Mellin transform therefore identify the actual completed trace with pi^(-s) Gamma(s) (4 pi/(ab))^(-(s+k-1)) Gamma(s+k-1) times its genuine Rankin convolution series.

Both sides are proved analytic throughout Re(s)>1, so the real-axis equality extends to that half-plane. The literal lattice functional equation gives the reflected identity on Re(s)<0. For each d dividing Q the equal-level adapter retains the original cusp function at (Q/d)*d=Q. Summing the actual reflected components recovers the original completed Gamma0 cusp function with its Mobius factor mu(d)(Qd)^(-s). The dual coefficients come from the actual rescaled q-expansions, are real and nonnegative, have a proved linear mean, and converge absolutely at 1-s. No scalar general-level reflection law is assumed.

The remaining general-level work is quantitative: rearrange the retained completion powers into positive conductors, prove the polynomial contour bounds and finite-component Riesz transform, and apply the already constructed sharp kernel differences and actual deconvolution. DUB-16 remains OPEN. Deligne, unconditional Sato–Tate and the final modular application also remain OPEN; 15/20 gates remain accepted.

Positivity applies to the original convolution coefficients, while the signed dual amplitudes are controlled by their norms. The already proved actual Mobius inversion transfers the sharp error to the original normalized cusp squares, with the true Petersson residue. Its positivity is proved for every nonzero form.

No smoothness is assumed for this direction; the proved original raising expansion supplies smoothness afterward. The exact original smooth raising module therefore equals the genuine real K-finite module under its original Hilbert inclusion. ### Original smooth algebraic local representation and irreducibility — 8 October 2026

Six further production modules construct the actual smooth algebraic local representation. The original local level group is compact as the genuine continuous image of the full finite adelic level group, and open as its preimage under the continuous one-place insertion. The literal algebraic span of the original local cusp orbit lies in the original full algebraic cusp core; its proved finite smoothness supplies a genuine open local stabilizer for every vector. The actual local fixed orthogonal projector sends each core vector into its own finite compact orbit span, so it preserves every algebraic invariant subspace of that core. Any nonzero vector has a genuine local translate pairing nontrivially with the original generator. Projecting that translate and using the proved cyclic fixed-line theorem forces the original generator into every nonzero invariant subspace. Thus the actual algebraic core is irreducible without a closedness assumption. Restricting the original action to this core yields an explicit smooth representation, nonzero for the original primitive cusp form, and a public theorem proves Mathlib’s IsIrreducible predicate for that genuine representation at every good prime.

The original good-prime local cyclic Hilbert representation and its actual smooth algebraic orbit representation are both proved irreducible. Every vector of the latter has a genuine open stabilizer. Finite dimensionality of invariants under every compact open subgroup, identification with all smooth vectors of the completion, spherical representation/Euler-factor classification and local/global factor compatibility still require proofs. The automorphic non-CM bridge and higher symmetric automorphy/continuation remain open. Eleven further production modules construct the full original adelic action on the genuine restricted good-place tensor with a fixed real-and-bad-place base. Removing finitely many good coordinates leaves a genuine good tail; beyond its actual local-level exceptions that tail fixes the original cusp reference and every finite tensor image. A proved stage bound therefore permits an explicit action recipe: enlarge the input by original unit references, apply the original coordinatewise local action and canonical base action, then include the result in the genuine directed quotient. Original finite-stage equivariance proves compatibility with every reference transition. This actual recipe descends through the directed quotient; its group laws and preservation of the independently descended tensor norm follow from the proved original isometry. The isometric operators extend to the Hilbert completion. The resulting genuine external action is equivariant with the original full adelic representation on every vector, is strongly continuous in the original adelic topology, and makes the completed tensor equivalence equivariant. The tensor action is constructed from the finite external recipe; no tensor action, equivariance or representation-factorization certificate is assumed.

The genuine restricted good-place tensor now carries a separately constructed full external adelic action, with exact original algebraic and completed equivariance and strong continuity. Eight further production modules prove the closed spherical matrix-coefficient formula for the original primitive local representation and uniqueness of the entire unit spherical function from its genuine Hecke eigenvalue. The genuine intrinsic Hecke boundary equation and recurrence uniquely force this formula for every original radial coefficient; actual local Cartan decomposition gives the corresponding formula at every local group element. The literal Fourier Satake roots give the complete homogeneous trace formula without dividing by their difference, covering repeated roots. The proved primitive prime-power recurrence identifies those traces with the original normalized Fourier coefficients. Normalizing the original nonzero cusp generator by its actual Hilbert norm makes its genuine unit-reference spherical coefficient exactly the same polynomial. Independently, the generic local Hecke and Cartan arguments apply to any actual unitary representation of the same rational p-adic general-linear group with trivial central character and an integral-fixed unit Hecke eigenvector. Two such vectors with the same normalized eigenvalue have identical full spherical functions, hence identical full orbit Gram matrices. These are statements about the actual local matrix group, coset trace, original vectors and inner products; no spherical coefficient formula or Gram identity is postulated.

### Continuous original Dirichlet idele characters and local self-twist consequence — 8 October 2026

Ten further production modules construct genuine continuous Dirichlet characters on the original rational ideles and prove a local twisted-intertwiner Fourier consequence. The existing positive-rational/integral-unit factorization is proved unique: a positive rational integral unit is one, so both factors are uniquely determined. This gives the actual multiplicative integral normalization, its retraction on original integral units and its triviality on positive principal rational ideles. Composing the original residue map and inverse Dirichlet value constructs the finite character. An explicit open principal congruence neighborhood proves continuity. The real sign homomorphism supplies the genuine continuous real component. The original real/finite idele coordinates assemble a continuous full character; the two parity values cancel at minus one, proving triviality on every principal rational unit. Independently, the actual scalar character twist is a group representation, and a genuine twisted intertwiner preserves fixed vectors where the actual character is trivial. Universal deformation representability, associated Galois representations, higher symmetric automorphic lifts and analytic continuation remain open, as do unconditional Deligne, non-CM Sato–Tate and the final modular application. ### Original fixed determinant, continuous first cohomology and restriction — 9 October 2026

Four further production modules derive the exact first-order determinant from Mathlib’s actual determinant expansion. Every original invertible dual-number matrix factors into its strict infinitesimal matrix and the constant inclusion of its original reduction; its determinant has the original determinant as ordinary part and the exact logarithmic trace correction as epsilon part. The original lift has fixed determinant if and only if every original cocycle value has trace zero, giving an explicit equivalence of those fibers. Every original adjoint coboundary has trace zero. The actual continuous adjoint cocycles form a submodule, all actual coboundaries are proved continuous, and their quotient defines genuine continuous degree-one adjoint cohomology. Its equality relation agrees with equality of those classes in the original algebraic H1. Strict conjugacy of original continuous lifts is equivalent to equality in this continuous quotient, and every continuous class is represented by an actual continuous lift. Restriction along the original continuous group homomorphism preserves actual cocycles and coboundaries, descends to a linear map on the genuine continuous quotient and sends each original lift class to the class of its actual restricted representation.

The continuous cohomology construction is the actual degree-one adjoint cocycle quotient. No higher continuous cohomology, Galois-specific finiteness or Selmer vanishing, local p-adic Hodge condition, or universal deformation representability is inferred. Associated Galois representations, higher symmetric automorphic lifts and analytic continuation remain open, as do unconditional Deligne, non-CM Sato–Tate and the final modular application. DUB-01–16 remain accepted; DUB-17–20 remain OPEN.

### Original representation coordinate algebra and its actual universal property — 9 October 2026

Four further production modules construct the actual polynomial coordinate algebra of matrix representations of the original group: one variable for every original matrix entry, with the ideal generated by the literal identity and multiplication equations. These equations are proved in the quotient. Its actual coordinate matrices form a genuine group homomorphism and hence are invertible, giving the actual universal representation. Evaluation at an original representation kills the entire relation ideal and descends to an algebra map; base change of the universal representation recovers that entire original representation. Conversely, evaluating a representation obtained from an original algebra map recovers the whole original algebra map. These two proved inverse constructions give a genuine equivalence over every coefficient algebra, and the coordinate evaluation is natural under coefficient-algebra maps.

This is algebraic representability of framed matrix representations of an abstract group by a concrete, potentially infinitely generated coordinate algebra. It does not prove complete local or Noetherian deformation representability for profinite groups, continuity of the universal representation, pseudodeformation representability, or a quotient by change of basis. Galois association, local p-adic Hodge and Selmer inputs, patching, higher symmetric automorphic lifts and analytic continuation remain open, as do unconditional Deligne, non-CM Sato–Tate and the final modular application. DUB-01–16 remain accepted; DUB-17–20 remain OPEN.

### Original finite quotients and symmetric-power parameter maps — 9 October 2026

Four further production modules prove that the actual kernel of an original continuous matrix representation over a discrete coefficient ring is open. For an original compact group, its actual kernel quotient is finite; the faithful quotient representation composes with the original quotient map to recover the original representation exactly. The concrete coordinate algebra of a finite group over a Noetherian coefficient ring is Noetherian by the pinned polynomial and quotient results. Consequently, each original continuous discrete representation has its exact evaluation from the actual Noetherian coordinate algebra of its own kernel quotient. Separately, evaluation of the actual symmetric power of the original universal rank-two representation constructs the genuine contravariant map on coordinate algebras. Its universal and original-evaluation identities are proved from the existing coefficient-change theorem. The actual symmetric power of a genuine first-order lift reduces correctly, preserves original strict conjugacy and equality of the original adjoint H1 classes, and retains continuity in the original topology.

The finite kernel quotient may vary with the original representation; no single finite quotient for all deformations is claimed. These finite-group coordinate algebras and genuine symmetric-power maps do not prove complete local profinite deformation or pseudodeformation representability, local p-adic Hodge conditions, Selmer finiteness or vanishing, Galois association, patching, higher automorphic lifts or analytic continuation. Unconditional Deligne, non-CM Sato–Tate and the final modular application remain open. DUB-01–16 remain accepted; DUB-17–20 remain OPEN.
